\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{t}^{\alpha}\big|\langle f(t),\phi\rangle\big|\,&\lesssim_{\alpha}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-(1-\alpha)p^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{1/p^{\prime}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big|\langle f(s),\phi\rangle\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{1/p}\\
&\lesssim t^{\alpha-1/p}\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\|\phi\|_{\Psi^{-1}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}-2p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}\end{split}

(4.5)

where
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{p-1}
(the second inequality follows from (
2.13
) and that
(1-\alpha)p^{\prime}<1
because
\alpha>\frac{1}{p}
).

(
4.5
) and (
2.13
) also imply that

\displaystyle\sup_{0<t\leq T}\frac{|\langle u(t)-u_{0},\phi\rangle|}{t^{\alpha-1/p}}\lesssim\sup_{0<t\leq T}\frac{I_{t}^{\alpha}|\langle f(t),\phi\rangle|}{t^{\alpha-1/p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\|\phi\|_{H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

(4.6)

This shows that
u(0):=u_{0}
satisfying (
4.3
) is uniquely determined as
\lim_{t\searrow 0}u(t)
.

For the uniqueness of
f
in (
4.3
), observe that if
I_{t}^{\alpha}\langle f(t),\phi\rangle=0
for all
t\in(0,T]
, then
I_{t}^{1}\langle f(t),\phi\rangle=0
for all
t\in(0,T]
.
Hence
\langle f(t),\phi\rangle=0
for almost every
t\in(0,T]
, and therefore
f=0
in
\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)
.

The following lemma shows a relation between (
4.2
) and (
4.3
).
In particular, if
\alpha>\frac{1}{p}
, then

\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)=\Big\{u\in\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)\,:\,u(0)=0\Big\}\,.

Lemma 4.4
.

(1)

Let
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
, and assume that
u(t),u_{0}\in\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
for
t\in(0,T]
, and that
\langle f(\cdot),\phi\rangle\in L_{1}((0,T))
.
If equation (
4.3
) holds for almost every
t\in(0,T]
, then for any
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T))
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big(I_{t}^{1-\alpha}\langle u(t)-u_{0},\phi\rangle\Big)\eta^{\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=-\int_{0}^{T}\langle f(t),\phi\rangle\eta(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,.

(4.7)

(2)

Let
0<\frac{1}{p}<\alpha\leq 1
.
Suppose that
u\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega)
,
u_{0}\in\Psi B_{p,\theta+2}^{\gamma+2-2/(\alpha p)}(\Omega)
, and
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)
satisfy (
4.7
) for all
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,T)\big)
.
Then there exists a unique
\overline{u}\in\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
such that
\overline{u}=u
almost everywhere on
(0,T]
,
\overline{u}(0)=u_{0}
, and
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\overline{u}=f
.

Note that in Lemma
4.4
.(1),
\langle f(\cdot),\phi\rangle\in L_{1}((0,T))
implies that

I_{t}^{1-\alpha}I_{t}^{\alpha}|\langle f(t),\phi\rangle|=I_{t}^{1}|\langle f(t),\phi\rangle|<\infty\,.

Hence
I_{t}^{\alpha}\langle f(t),\phi\rangle
is well-defined for almost every
t
.

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

(1) Equation (
4.7
) follows directly from (
4.3
) and (
2.1
) by straightforward calculation.

(2) Define
\overline{u}(t):=u_{0}+I_{t}^{\alpha}f(t)
in the sense of distributions,
i.e.
,

\displaystyle\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\rangle:=\langle u_{0},\phi\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(t-s)^{-1+\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\langle f(s),\phi\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.8)

By Remark
4.3
.(2),
\overline{u}(t)
is well-defined in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
for any
t\in(0,T]
.

We first claim that
\langle u(t),\phi\rangle=\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\rangle
for almost every
t\in(0,T]
.
For arbitrary fixed
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,T)\big)
, put

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}(t):=\int_{t}^{T}\frac{(r-t)^{-1+\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\eta(r)\,\mathrm{d}r\,.

Then
\widetilde{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T))
, and

\partial_{t}^{k}\widetilde{\eta}(t)=\int_{t}^{T}\frac{(r-t)^{-1+\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)}(\partial_{t}^{k}\eta)(r)\,\mathrm{d}r
.
It follows from (
2.1
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big(I_{t}^{1-\alpha}\langle u(t)-u_{0},\phi\rangle\Big)\widetilde{\eta}\,^{\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=-\int_{0}^{T}\langle u(r)-u_{0},\phi\rangle\eta(r)\,\mathrm{d}r\,.

On the other hand, (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) imply that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big(I_{t}^{1-\alpha}\langle u(t)-u_{0},\phi\rangle\Big)\widetilde{\eta}\,^{\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\langle f(t),\phi\rangle\widetilde{\eta}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{T}\langle\overline{u}(t)-u_{0},\phi\rangle\eta(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,.

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\langle u(t)-u_{0},\phi\rangle\eta(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{T}\Big(I_{t}^{1-\alpha}\langle u(t)-u_{0},\phi\rangle\Big)\widetilde{\eta}\,^{\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\langle\overline{u}(t)-u_{0},\phi\rangle\eta(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,.

Since this equality holds for all
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,T)\big)
, we conclude that
\langle u(t),\phi\rangle=\langle\overline{u}(t),\phi\rangle
for almost every
t\in(0,T]
.

It follows from the separability of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
that
u(t)=\overline{u}(t)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
for almost every
t\in(0,T]
, which implies that
\overline{u}\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
.
By (
4.8
),
\overline{u}\in\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
with
\overline{u}(0)=u_{0}
and
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\overline{u}=f
.

To prove the uniqueness of
\overline{u}
, consider the case that
\overline{u}\in\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
with
\overline{u}(0)=0
and
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\overline{u}=0
.
Then, by (
4.6
),
\overline{u}(t)=0
for all
t\in(0,T]
.
Therefore the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
,
1<p<\infty
, and
0<T<\infty
.

(1)

\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma}(\Omega,T)
is a Banach space, and
C_{c}^{\infty}\big((0,\infty)\times\Omega\big)
is dense in
\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.

(2)

Suppose that
\alpha>\frac{1}{p}
. Then

\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma}(\Omega,T)
is a Banach space, and
C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,\infty)\times\Omega\big)
is dense in
\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.

Proof.

It follows from (
4.4
)–(
4.6
) that both
\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)
and
\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)
are Banach spaces.
Therefore, it remains to prove that
C_{c}^{\infty}\big((0,\infty)\times\Omega\big)
and
C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,\infty)\times\Omega\big)
are dense in
\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
and
\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
, respectively.

(1) Let
\delta>0
be given.
By Lemma
2.16
, there exists
i_{0},j_{0},k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\|u-\Lambda_{i_{0},j_{0},k_{0}}u\|_{\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}<\delta/2\,,

(4.9)

where
\Lambda_{i_{0},j_{0},k_{0}}
is the operator defined in Lemma
2.16
.
Set
v:=\Lambda_{i_{0},j_{0},k_{0}}u
and
g:=\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v=\Lambda_{i_{0},j_{0},k_{0}}(\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u)
.
Observe that
v\in\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.
For convenience, for
t<0
, we put
v(t)=0
.
Take
\eta_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{0})\subset[1/2,1)
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{0}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=1
.
For
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we define

v^{(\epsilon)}(t):=\int_{0}^{1}v(t-s)\eta_{0}(\epsilon s)\,\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{and}\quad g^{(\epsilon)}(t):=\int_{0}^{1}g(t-s)\eta_{0}(\epsilon s)\,\mathrm{d}s\,.

Then
v^{(\epsilon)}(t)=0
for all
t\in(0,\epsilon/2)
.
It is easy to see that
v^{(\epsilon)}\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
with
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\big(v^{(\epsilon)}\big)=g^{(\epsilon)}
.
Since
v\in\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
, the definition of
v^{(\epsilon)}
implies that
v^{(\epsilon)}\rightarrow v
as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
in
\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.
In the same manner,
g^{(\epsilon)}\rightarrow g
in
\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
Therefore, we conclude that there exists
\epsilon>0
such that
\|v-v^{(\epsilon)}\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}<\delta/2
.
By combining this with (
4.9
), we have
\|u-v^{(\epsilon)}\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}<\delta
.
It follows from the definition of
v^{(\epsilon)}
that
v^{(\epsilon)}
lie in
C_{c}^{\infty}\big((0,T]\big)
.
Therefore the proof is complete.

(2) The proof of this assertion is almost the same with the proof of (1) of this lemma (for
t<0
, put
v(t)=v(0)
instead of
v(t)\equiv 0
).
We leave the details to the reader.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\Psi^{\prime}
be a regular Harnack function,
p^{\prime}\in(1,\infty)
, and
\gamma^{\prime},\,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)\cap\Psi^{\prime}\mathbb{H}_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega,T)
, then for any
\epsilon>0
, there exist
g\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\Omega)
such that

\|f-g\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\|f-g\|_{\Psi^{\prime}\mathbb{H}_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega,T)}<\epsilon\,.

Proof.

Since
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
and
\Psi^{\prime}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)
are separable Banach spaces, by a standard mollification argument, it suffices to consider the case where
f
is a
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\cap\Psi^{\prime}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)
-valued continuous function in
[0,T]
.
In this case, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists a sufficiently large
N\in\mathbb{N}
and functions
\eta_{1},\,\ldots,\,\eta_{N}\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big((0,T)\big)
such that

\|\,f-\widetilde{f}\,\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\|\,f-\widetilde{f}\,\|_{\Psi^{\prime}\mathbb{H}_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega,T)}<\epsilon\,,

where

\widetilde{f}(t,\cdot)=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\eta_{k}(t)f\big(kT/N,\,\cdot\,\big)\,.

Since
C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\Omega)
is dense in
\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)\cap\Psi^{\prime}\mathbb{H}_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega,T)
(see Lemma
2.16
), this completes the proof.
∎

We conclude this subsection with the following parabolic embedding theorem for
\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
.

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
\alpha>\frac{1}{p}
and
\frac{1}{p}\leq\beta<\alpha
.
Then, for any
u\in\Psi\mathcal{H}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
and
0\leq s<t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\|u(t)-u(s)\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta+2p\beta/\alpha}^{\gamma+2-2\beta/\alpha}(\Omega)}\leq N|t-s|^{\beta-1/p}\|u\|_{\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}\,,

(4.10)

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,\alpha,\beta)
.
In addition,

\displaystyle\|u(t)-u(s)\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N|t-s|^{\alpha-1/p}\|u\|_{\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}\,,

(4.11)

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,\alpha)
.

Proof.

For any
\gamma^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, the map
f\mapsto\Psi^{-1}f
is an isometric isomorphism from
\Psi H_{p,\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)
(resp.
\Psi B_{p,\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)
,
\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega,T)
) to
H_{p,\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)
(resp.
B_{p,\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)
,
\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega,T)
).
Therefore, it suffices to prove the proposition for the case
\Psi\equiv 1
.
The proof of this case is almost the same as that of
[
42
, Theorem 7.1]
.
Since
u\in\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
, the function

u_{n}(t,x):=u(t,\mathrm{e}^{n}x)\zeta_{0,(n)}(\mathrm{e}^{n}x)\in\mathcal{H}_{p}^{\gamma+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)

satisfies

\big(\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u_{n}\big)(s,\cdot)=\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u(s,\mathrm{e}^{n}\,\cdot\,)\zeta_{0,(n)}(\mathrm{e}^{n}\,\cdot)

in the sense of distributions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Since
u_{n}\in\mathcal{H}_{p}^{\gamma+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)
, by (
B.4
) with
A:=\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}
, we obtain, for
\beta\in[1/p,1)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{e}^{2np\beta/\alpha}\|u_{n}(t)-u_{n}(s)\|_{B_{p}^{\gamma+2-2\beta/\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\\
\leq N&|t-s|^{p\beta-1}\big(\|u_{n}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}^{p}+\mathrm{e}^{2np}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}^{p}+\mathrm{e}^{2/\alpha}\|u_{0}\|_{B_{p}^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\big)\,,\end{split}

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\beta)
.
This yields that

\displaystyle\|u(t)-u(s)\|_{B_{p,\theta+2p\beta/\alpha}^{\gamma+2-2\beta/\alpha}(\Omega)}^{p}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{n(\theta+2p\beta)}\|u_{n}(t)-u_{n}(s)\|_{B_{p}^{\gamma+2-2\beta/\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|t-s|^{\beta p-1}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{n\theta}\Big(\|u_{n}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}^{p}+\mathrm{e}^{2np}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}^{p}+\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}\|u_{0}\|_{B_{p}^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|t-s|^{\beta p-1}\Big(\|u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}^{p}+\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}^{p}+\|u_{0}\|_{B_{p,\theta+2/\alpha}^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}(\Omega)}^{p}\Big)\,,

which means (
4.10
).
Estimate (
4.11
) follows in the same way, using (
B.5
) in place of (
B.4
).
∎

4.2.
Solvability of parabolic equations

We now state the main result of this paper.
Let
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
0<T<\infty
, and consider the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u+f\quad\text{in}\,\,\,(0,T],

(4.12)

where
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
,
0<\nu_{1}\leq\nu_{2}<\infty
(see Section
3
for the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
).

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
\Omega
be an open set admitting the Hardy inequality (
1.3
) and let
\psi
be a superharmonic Harnack function in
\Omega
with its regularization
\Psi
.
Then for any
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\mu\in\mathcal{I}(\psi,p,\nu_{1}/\nu_{2})
, and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, the following statements hold:

(1)

For any
f\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)
, equation (
4.12
)
has a unique solution
u
in
\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,d-2}(\Omega,T)
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,d-2}(\Omega,T)}\leq N\|f\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)}\,,

(4.13)

where
N=N(d,\alpha,p,\gamma,\mu,\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega),\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{4})
.

(2)

If
\frac{1}{p}<\alpha
, then for any
u_{0}\in\Psi^{\mu}B^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}_{p,d+2/\alpha-2}(\Omega)
and
f\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)
, equation (
4.12
) with
u(0,\cdot)=u_{0}

has a unique solution
u
in
\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,d-2}(\Omega,T)
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,d-2}(\Omega,T)}\leq N\left(\|u_{0}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}B^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}_{p,d+2/\alpha-2}(\Omega,T)}+\|f\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)}\right)\,,

(4.14)

where
N=N(d,\alpha,p,\gamma,\mu,\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega),\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{4})
.

Recall that
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
denotes the constant in (
1.3
),
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi)
and
\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\Psi)
denote the constants in Definition
2.6
, and
\mathrm{C}_{4}
denotes the constant in Definition
3.1
.

Remark 4.9
.

Theorem
4.8
can be reformulated without explicitly including
\Psi
, by setting
\Psi=\widetilde{\psi}
, where
\widetilde{\psi}
is the function given in Lemma
2.8
.(1).
In addition, when
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the equivalent norms of
\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)
and
\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+s}(\Omega)
,
s\in(0,1)
, are given in (
2.10
) and Proposition
A.1
, respectively.
For equivalent norms in the case where
-\gamma\in\mathbb{N}
, use (
2.28
).
For
p=2
and
\alpha=1
, one may also use the identity
\Psi^{\mu}B_{2,d}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)=\Psi^{\mu}H_{2,d}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)
(see (
2.19
)).

To prove Theorem
4.8
, we need the help of the following three lemmas:

Lemma 4.10
.

Suppose that
u\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+1}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)
satisfies
f:=\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}u\in\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)
.
Then
u\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)
, and

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}\leq N\left(\|u\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+1}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}+\|f\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}\right)

(4.15)

where
N=N(d,p,\alpha,\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
.
In particular,
N
is independent of
T
.

Proof.

By considering
v:=(1-\Delta)^{\gamma/2}u
and
g:=(1-\Delta)^{\gamma/2}f
, it suffices to prove the estimate for
\gamma=0
.
Let
\gamma=0
.
Considering the equation
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u-u+(u+f)
,
(
4.15
) follows from
[
41
, Theorem 1.2]
for
\alpha=1
, and from
[
13
, Theorem 2.1]
for
0<\alpha<1
.
∎

Lemma 4.11

(Higher order estimates)
.

Let
u\in\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
for some
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and let
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)
.
Suppose that (
4.2
) holds, where
f
in (
4.2
) is replaced by
\mathcal{L}u+f
.
Then
u
belongs to
\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
with
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u+f
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|u\|_{\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}\leq N\left(\|u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}+\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)}\right)\,,\end{split}

(4.16)

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),s)
.

Proof.

Step 1.
First, we consider the case
s\geq\gamma+1
.
Since

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim_{d,p,s,\gamma}\|u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{s}(\Omega,T)}

(see (
2.20
)), it suffices to prove the case
s=\gamma+1
.
Recall the relation between
\zeta_{0}
and the space
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}
given in Definition
2.10
.
Put

\displaystyle v_{n}(t,x)=\zeta_{0}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(\mathrm{e}^{n}x)\big)\Phi(\mathrm{e}^{n}x)u(\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}t,\mathrm{e}^{n}x)\,,

where
\Phi=\Psi^{-1}
, so that
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Phi)
depends only on
d
and
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi)
.
Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{n(\theta+2/\alpha)}\left\|v_{n}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)}^{p}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{n\theta}\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\zeta_{0}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(\mathrm{e}^{n}\cdot)\big)\Phi(\mathrm{e}^{n}\cdot)u(t,\mathrm{e}^{n}\cdot)\right\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.17)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\|\Phi u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega,T)}^{p}=\|u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega,T)}^{p}<\infty\,.

This implies that
v_{n}\in\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)
.
In the same manner as in (
4.17
),
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v_{n}
is well-defined in the sense of (
4.2
) and belongs to
\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)
.
Therefore we have
v_{n}\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p}^{\alpha,\gamma+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v_{n}=\mathcal{L}_{n}v_{n}+\widetilde{f}_{n}\,,\end{split}

where
\mathcal{L}_{n}v(t,\cdot):=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}a^{ij}(\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}t)D_{ij}v(t,\cdot)
, and

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n}(t,x):=

\displaystyle\,\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}\Phi\,f\big)(\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}t,\mathrm{e}^{n}x)+\mathrm{e}^{2n}\Big[\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}\Phi\,\mathcal{L}u\big)-\mathcal{L}\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}\Phi u\big)\Big](\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}t,\mathrm{e}^{n}x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}\Phi\,f\big)(\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}t,\mathrm{e}^{n}x)-\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}a^{ij}(\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}t)F_{n,ij}(\mathrm{e}^{2n/\alpha}t,\mathrm{e}^{n}x)\,;

\displaystyle F_{n,ij}:=

\displaystyle\,\mathrm{e}^{2n}\zeta_{0,(n)}\Big(2D_{j}\Phi\cdot D_{i}u+D_{ij}\Phi\cdot u\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{e}^{n}\big(\zeta_{0}^{\prime}\big)_{(n)}\Big(2D_{i}\widetilde{\rho}\cdot D_{j}(\Phi u)+D_{ij}\widetilde{\rho}\cdot\Phi u\Big)+\big(\zeta_{0}^{\prime\prime}\big)_{(n)}D_{i}\widetilde{\rho}\cdot D_{j}\widetilde{\rho}\cdot\Phi u\,.

Using (
2.27
) and (
2.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{n(\theta+2/\alpha)}\big\|\widetilde{f}_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi f\right\|^{p}_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\left\|\Phi_{x}u_{x}\right\|^{p}_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\left\|\Phi_{xx}u\right\|^{p}_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}

(4.18)

\displaystyle+\left\|\widetilde{\rho}_{x}(\Phi u)_{x}\right\|^{p}_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\left\|\widetilde{\rho}_{xx}\Phi u\right\|^{p}_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\left\|\widetilde{\rho}_{x}\widetilde{\rho}_{x}\Phi u\right\|^{p}_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\|\Phi f\|^{p}_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\|\Phi u\|^{p}_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}=\|f\|^{p}_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\|u\|^{p}_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}<\infty\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.
Hence
\tilde{f}_{n}\in\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

By Lemma
4.10
, we have

\displaystyle\|v_{n}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)}\lesssim_{N}\|v_{n}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+1}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)}+\|\widetilde{f}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)}\,,

(4.19)

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
.
Combining (
4.17
), (
4.19
), and (
4.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Phi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{n(\theta+2)}\|v_{n}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{n(\theta+2)}\left(\|v_{n}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+1}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)}^{p}+\|\widetilde{f}_{n}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathrm{e}^{-2n/\alpha}T)}^{p}\right)

(4.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}^{p}+\|f\|^{p}_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\,.

Noting that (
4.2
) holds for
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u:=\mathcal{L}u+f
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\leq\,&\|\mathcal{L}u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\\
\lesssim\,&\|u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}+\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\end{split}

(4.21)

(see (
2.27
)).
These yield
u\in\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.
In addition, (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) imply (
4.16
) for
s=\gamma+1
.
Therefore the proof for
s=\gamma+1
is complete.

Step 2.
Recall that the case
s\geq\gamma+1
was proved in Step 1.
For
s<\gamma+1
, take
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\gamma+1-k\leq s<\gamma+2-k\,,

and repeatedly apply the result in Step 1 with
(s,\gamma)
replaced by
(s,\gamma-k)
,
(\gamma-k,\gamma+1-k)
, …,
(\gamma-1,\gamma)
.
Then we obtain
u\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}
and

\displaystyle\|\Phi u\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Phi u\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}+\|\Phi f\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,\,\,\,\cdots\,\,\,\,

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Phi u\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma-k}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}+\|\Phi f\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Phi u\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}+\|\Phi f\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\,.

Therefore the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 4.12
.

Suppose the following holds:

For any
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, equation (
4.12
) has a unique solution
u
in
\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}\leq N_{\gamma}\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\,,

(4.22)

where
N_{\gamma}
is a constant independent of
u_{0}
,
f
, and
u
.

Then for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the following holds:

For any
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s}(\Omega,T)
, equation (
4.12
) has a unique solution
u
in
\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,s+2}(\Omega,T)
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,s+2}(\Omega,T)}\leq N_{s}\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s}(\Omega,T)}\,,

(4.23)

where
N_{s}
is a constant depending only on
d,\,p,\,\gamma,\,\theta,\,\nu_{1},\,\nu_{2},\,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\,N_{\gamma},\,s
.

Proof.

To prove uniqueness, suppose that
\overline{u}\in\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,s+2}(\Omega,T)
satisfies
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\overline{u}=\mathcal{L}\overline{u}
and
\overline{u}(0,\cdot)\equiv 0
.
By Lemma
4.11
,
\overline{u}
belongs to
\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.
By the uniqueness of solutions in the assumption of this lemma,
\overline{u}(t)\equiv 0
for almost every
t\in(0,T]
.
Therefore, uniqueness of solutions follows.
Thus, it remains to show the existence of solutions and estimate (
4.23
).

Step 1.
We first consider the case
s>\gamma
.
Let
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)
.
Since
\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)\subset\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)
, it follows that
f
belongs to
\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)
, and hence there exists a solution
u\in\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
of equation (
4.12
).
It follows from Lemma
4.11
, (
4.22
), and (
2.20
) that

\displaystyle\left\|u\right\|_{\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,s+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\left\|u\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}+\left\|f\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\leq N_{\gamma}\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}(N_{\gamma}+1)\left\|f\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,\gamma,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),s)
.
Therefore
u
belongs to
\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,s+2}(\Omega,T)
, and the proof is complete.

Step 2.
Consider the case
s<\gamma
. By mathematical induction, it suffices to show that if this lemma holds for
s=s_{0}+1
, then it also holds for
s=s_{0}
.
(Recall that the case
s\geq\gamma
was proved in Step 1.)

Let us assume that this lemma holds for
s=s_{0}+1
.
For
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}}(\Omega,T)
, by Lemma
2.14
, there exist functions
f^{0}\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}+1}(\Omega,T)
and
f^{1},\,\ldots,\,f^{d}\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+p}^{s_{0}+1}(\Omega,T)
such that
f=f^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}
and

\displaystyle\left\|f^{0}\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}+1}(\Omega,T)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-1}f^{i}\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}+1}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim_{N}\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}}(\Omega,T)}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,s_{0},\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.
By the induction hypothesis, there exist
v^{0},\,\cdots,\,v^{d}\in\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,s_{0}+3}(\Omega,T)
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v^{0}=\mathcal{L}v^{0}+f^{0}\quad\text{and}\quad\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v^{i}=\mathcal{L}v^{i}+\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-1}f^{i}\quad\text{for }i=1,\,\ldots,\,d\,,

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{d}\left\|v^{i}\right\|_{\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N_{s_{0}+1}\bigg(\left\|f^{0}\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-1}f^{i}\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\bigg)

(4.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle N_{s_{0}+1}\|f\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}}(\Omega)}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,s_{0},\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.
Put
v=v^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}\big(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i}\big)
, and observe that

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}v+f-\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}\big(\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\mathcal{L}v^{i}\big)\,.

Using (
2.27
) and (
2.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|D_{i}\left(\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\mathcal{L}v^{i}\right)\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\|\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\mathcal{L}v^{i}\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+2}_{p,\theta+p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\big\|\widetilde{\rho}_{xx}v^{i}\big\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+2}_{p,\theta+p}(\Omega)}+\big\|\widetilde{\rho}_{x}v^{i}_{x}\big\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+2}_{p,\theta+p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\|v^{i}\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}<\infty\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,s_{0},\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
.
By the induction hypothesis, there exists
w\in\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
such that

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}w=\mathcal{L}w+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}\big(\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\mathcal{L}v^{i}\big)\quad(=\mathcal{L}v-f)\,.

Such
w
satisfies

\displaystyle\|w\|_{\Psi\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N_{s_{0}+1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\|D_{i}\left(\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\mathcal{L}v^{i}\right)\right\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}

(4.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle N_{s_{0}+1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|v^{i}\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,s_{0},\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
.
Put
u=v+w=v^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})+w
.
Then
u
satisfies
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u+f
.
Moreover, by (
4.24
) and (
4.25
), we obtain (
4.23
) for
s=s_{0}
.
∎

Now, we provide the proof of Theorem
4.8
.

Proof of Theorem
4.8
.

(1) By Lemma
4.12
, it suffices to prove the case
\gamma=0
.

A priori
estimates.
Make use of Theorem
3.5
and (
2.10
) to obtain that for any
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big((0,T]\times\Omega\big)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d-2}(\Omega,T)}&\lesssim_{N}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)}\,,\end{split}

where
N=N(d,\alpha,p,\mu,\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega),\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{4})
.
Combining this with Lemma
4.11
, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d-2}(\Omega,T)}+\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)}

(4.26)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)}\,.

By Lemma
4.5
, (
4.26
) also holds for all
u\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)
.
Therefore the
a priori
estimates follow.
The uniqueness of solutions also follows from (
4.26
).

Existence of solutions.

We first consider the case
\mathcal{L}=\nu_{1}\Delta
.
For a fixed
f\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)
, Lemma
4.6
implies that there exists a sequence
\big(f^{(n)}\big)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T]\times\Omega)
such that
f^{(n)}\rightarrow f
in
\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)
.
Make use of Lemmas
3.6
and
4.11
(for
\gamma=2
and
s=0
) to obtain that there exists a solution
u^{(n)}\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)
of the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u^{(n)}=\nu_{1}\Delta u^{(n)}+f^{(n)}\,.

(4.27)

By (
4.26
),

\displaystyle\|u^{(n)}-u^{(m)}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\|\big(\partial_{t}^{\alpha}-\nu_{1}\Delta\big)\big(u^{(n)}-u^{(m)}\big)\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|f^{(n)}-f^{(m)}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)}\,.

This implies that
\big(u^{(n)}\big)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)
.
Since
\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)
is a Banach space, there exists
u\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)
such that
u^{(n)}\rightarrow u
in
\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)
.
Letting
n\rightarrow\infty
in (
4.27
), we have
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\nu_{1}\Delta u=f
.
Therefore the theorem is proved for the case
\mathcal{L}=\nu_{1}\Delta
.

Now consider a general operator
\mathcal{L}:=\sum_{i,j}a^{ij}D_{ij}\in\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
.
For
s\in[0,1]
, put

\mathcal{L}_{s}:=(1-s)\nu_{1}\Delta+s\mathcal{L}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\big((1-s)\nu_{1}\delta^{ij}+sa^{ij}\big)D_{ij}\,.

For any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\nu_{1}|\xi|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\big((1-s)\nu_{1}\delta^{ij}+sa^{ij}(t)\big)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\leq\nu_{2}|\xi|^{2}\,,

(4.28)

which implies that
\mathcal{L}_{s}\in\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
.
It follows from (
4.26
) that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)}\leq N\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}_{s}u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{L}_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega,T)}

for all
u\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,T)
and
s\in[0,1]
. Here,
N
is the constant in (
4.26
), which is independent of
s
.
By the method of continuity (see,
e.g.
,
[
19
, Theorem 5.2]
), the unique solvability for
\mathcal{L}_{0}
yields that for
\mathcal{L}_{1}:=\mathcal{L}
.

(2) We first prove the existence of solutions.
By Lemma
A.2
, for
u_{0}\in\Psi^{\mu}B_{p,d+2/\alpha-2}^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}(\Omega)
, there exists
v\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
such that
v(0)=u_{0}
and

\displaystyle\|v\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}\leq N(d,p,\alpha,\gamma,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\mu)\|u_{0}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}B_{p,d+2/\alpha-2}^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}(\Omega)}\,.

(4.29)

By the definition of
\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
, we have
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v-\mathcal{L}v\in\mathbb{H}_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)
and

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v-\mathcal{L}v\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim_{d,\nu_{1},\nu_{2}}\|v\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}B_{p,d+2/\alpha-2}^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}(\Omega)}\,.

(4.30)

By (1) of this theorem and Lemma
4.4
.(2), there exists
w\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
such that

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}w=\mathcal{L}w+f-\big(\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v-\mathcal{L}v\big)\quad\text{in}\quad(0,T]\quad;\quad w(0,\cdot)=0\,.

Moreover, by (
4.13
) and (
4.30
),

\displaystyle\|w\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f-(\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v-\mathcal{L}v)\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\|u_{0}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}B_{p,d+2/\alpha-2}^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}(\Omega)}\,.

Put
u:=v+w
.
Then
u
is a solution of (
4.12
), and (
4.14
) follows from (
4.29
) and (
4.30
).

The uniqueness follows from (1) of this theorem.
Indeed, if
u\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
satisfies (
4.12
) with
f\equiv 0
and
u_{0}=0
, then Lemma
4.4
implies that
u\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
with
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}u=0
.
By the uniqueness of solutions in (1) of this theorem, we conclude that
u=0
in
\Psi^{\mu}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.
This implies that
\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}=0
(see Lemma
4.4
).
∎

We conclude this subsection by establishing the global uniqueness of solutions.

Theorem 4.13
.

Let (
1.3
) holds for
\Omega
and for each
k=1,\,2
, let

\begin{gathered}\text{$\psi_{k}$ is a superharmonic Harnack function in $\Omega$}\,\,,\quad\text{$\Psi_{k}$ is a regularization of $\psi_{k}$}\,\,,\\
\gamma_{k}\in\mathbb{R}\,\,,\,\,\,\,p_{k}\in(1,\infty)\,\,,\,\,\,\,\mu_{k}\in\mathcal{I}(\psi_{k},p_{k},\nu_{2}/\nu_{1})\,.\end{gathered}

Suppose that
f\in\bigcap_{k=1,2}\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathbb{H}_{p_{k},d+2p_{k}-2}^{\gamma_{k}}(\Omega,T)
.

(1)

For each
k=1,\,2
, let

u^{(k)}\in\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)
be the solution to the equation

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u^{(k)}=\mathcal{L}u^{(k)}+f\,.

Then
u^{(1)}(t)=u^{(2)}(t)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
for almost every
t\in[0,T]
.

(2)

Assume further that
p_{1},\,p_{2}>\frac{1}{\alpha}
and
u_{0}\in\bigcap_{k=1,2}\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}B_{p_{k},d+2/\alpha-2}^{\gamma_{k}+2-2/(\alpha p_{k})}(\Omega)
. For each
k=1,\,2
, let

u^{(k)}\in\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathcal{H}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)
be the solution to the equation

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u^{(k)}=\mathcal{L}u^{(k)}+f\quad;\quad u^{(k)}(0)=u_{0}\,.

Then
u^{(1)}(t)=u^{(2)}(t)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

(1) Define

\displaystyle\mathring{X}:=\bigcap_{k=1,2}\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)\quad\text{and}\quad Y:=\bigcap_{k=1,2}\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathbb{H}_{p_{k},d+2p-2}^{\gamma_{k}}(\Omega,T)\,,

(4.31)

and recall that
f\in Y
.

Step 1.
Consider the case
\mathcal{L}=\nu_{1}\Delta
.
Lemma
4.6
yields that there exists
f_{n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big((0,T]\times\Omega\big)
such that
f_{n}\rightarrow f
in
Y
.
Since
\mu_{k}\in\mathcal{I}(\psi_{k},p_{k},\nu_{2}/\nu_{1})\subset(-1/p_{k},1-1/p_{k})
, it follows from Lemmas
3.6
and
4.11
that there exists
u_{n}\in\mathring{X}
such that

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u_{n}=\nu_{1}\Delta u_{n}+f_{n}\,.

By Theorem
4.8
, we obtain that for each
k=1,\,2
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|u_{n}-u^{(k)}\|_{\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|f_{n}-f\|_{\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathbb{H}_{p_{k},d+2p-2}^{\gamma_{k}}(\Omega,T)}=0\,.

This implies that

u^{(1)}(t)=u^{(2)}(t)\quad\text{in}\,\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)\,\,,\,\,\,\,\text{for almost every}\,\,t\in[0,T]\,.

Therefore the case
\mathcal{L}=\nu_{1}\Delta
is proved.

Note that

u^{(1)}(\,\cdot\,)
(
=u^{(2)}(\,\cdot\,)
) is the unique solution of

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\nu_{1}\Delta u+f\,,

(4.32)

in the class
\mathring{X}
.
Indeed,
\Psi_{1}^{\,\mu_{1}}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p_{1},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{1}+2}(\Omega,T)
admits a unique solution to equation (
4.32
), and
\mathring{X}\subset\Psi_{1}^{\,\mu_{1}}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p_{1},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{1}+2}(\Omega,T)
.

Step 2.
Let
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
.
For
r\in[0,1]
, we denote
\mathcal{L}_{r}:=(1-r)\nu_{1}\Delta+r\mathcal{L}
.
By (
4.28
), Theorem
4.8
implies that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\mathring{X}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1,2}\|u\|_{\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)}

\displaystyle\leq N\sum_{k=1,2}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}_{r}u\|_{\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathbb{H}_{p_{k},d+2p-2}^{\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)}=N\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}_{r}u\|_{Y}

for all
u\in\mathring{X}
, where
N
is independent of
u
and
r\in[0,1]
.
In addition, by the result in Step 1, the map
u\mapsto\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u-\mathcal{L}_{0}u
is a bijective map from
\mathring{X}
to
Y
.
Therefore the method of continuity ensures that for any
f\in Y
, there exists a unique solution
u\in\mathring{X}
of the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u+f\,.

(4.33)

For each
k=1,\,2
,
u^{(k)}
is the unique solution of equation (
4.33
) in
\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)
.
Therefore
u=u^{(k)}
in
\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathcal{H}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)
.
Consequently,
u^{(1)}(t)=u(t)=u^{(2)}(t)
for almost every
t\in(0,T]
.

(2) Use the notations in (
4.31
), define

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}X:=\bigcap_{k=1,2}\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathcal{H}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)\quad\text{and}\quad Z:=\bigcap_{k=1,2}\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}B_{p_{k},d+2/\alpha-2}^{\gamma_{k}+2-2/(\alpha p_{k})}(\Omega)\,,\end{gathered}

and recall that
u_{0}\in Z
.
By Lemma
2.16
, there exists
u_{0,n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
such that
u_{0,n}\rightarrow u_{0}
in
Z
.
Recall that
T<\infty
, and hence

\|u_{0,n}\|_{\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathbb{H}_{p_{k},d+2p_{k}-2}^{\gamma_{k}}(\Omega,T)}=T^{1/p}\|u_{0,n}\|_{\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}H_{p_{k},d+2p_{k}-2}^{\gamma_{k}}(\Omega)}<\infty\,.

By (1) of this theorem, there exists
v_{n}\in\mathring{X}
such that

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v_{n}=\mathcal{L}v_{n}+f+\mathcal{L}u_{0,n}\,.

In view of Lemma
4.4
.(2), we regard
v_{n}
as an element of
X
with
v_{n}(0)=0
.
Put
u_{n}(t,\cdot)=v_{n}(t,\cdot)+u_{0,n}
.
Since
u_{0,n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
v_{n}\in X
,
u_{n}
also belongs to
X
.
Moreover, we have

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u_{n}-\mathcal{L}u_{n}=\big(\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v_{n}-\mathcal{L}v_{n}\big)+\big(\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u_{0,n}-\mathcal{L}u_{0,n}\big)=(f+\mathcal{L}u_{0,n})-\mathcal{L}u_{0,n}=f

and
u_{n}(0,\cdot)=v_{n}(0,\cdot)+u_{0,n}=u_{0,n}
.
Note that for each
k=1,\,2
,

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\big(u^{(k)}-u_{n}\big)=\mathcal{L}(u^{(k)}-u_{n}\big)\quad\text{in}\quad(0,T]\quad;\quad\big(u^{(k)}-u_{n}\big)(0,\cdot)=u_{0}-u_{0,n}\,.

By Theorem
4.8
.(2), we obtain that

\displaystyle\|u^{(k)}-u_{n}\|_{\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathcal{H}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim\|u_{0}-u_{0,n}\|_{\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}B_{p_{k},d+2/\alpha-2}^{\gamma_{k}+2-2/(\alpha p)}(\Omega)}\,.

This implies that for each
k=1,\,2
,
u_{n}
converges to
u^{(k)}
in
\Psi_{k}^{\,\mu_{k}}\mathcal{H}_{p_{k},d-2}^{\alpha,\gamma_{k}+2}(\Omega,T)
.
By (
4.6
), for any
t\in(0,T]
,
u_{n}(t)
converges to
u^{(k)}(t)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
.
Therefore we conclude that
u^{(1)}(t)=u^{(2)}(t)
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
for all
t\in(0,T]
.
∎

5.
Application

We apply Theorem
4.8
to various domain settings: the fat exterior condition, the thin exterior condition, convex domains, the totally vanishing exterior Reifenberg condition, and conic domains.
Notably, we also treat parabolic equations with time-measurable coefficients in the case where a domain
\Omega
is convex or satisfying the totally vanishing exterior Reifenberg condition.

Throughout this section, we assume that
\Omega
is a (possibly unbounded) domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
d\geq 2
.
Recall that
\rho(x)
denotes the boundary distance function
d(x,\partial\Omega)
, and
\widetilde{\rho}
is a regularization of
\rho
provided by Lemma
2.8
.(1).

We summarize basic properties of classical superharmonic functions.

Lemma 5.1
.

(1)

Let
\phi_{1},\,\phi_{2}
be classical superharmonic functions in
\Omega
. Then
\phi_{1}\wedge\phi_{2}
is also a classical superharmonic function in
\Omega
.

(2)

Let
\{\phi_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
be an arbitrary family of positive classical superharmonic functions in
\Omega
.
Then
\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\phi_{i}
is a superharmonic function in
\Omega
.

(3)

Let
\phi_{1},\,\phi_{2}
be positive classical superharmonic functions in
\Omega
. For any
s\in(0,1)
,
\phi_{1}^{s}\phi_{2}^{1-s}
is also a classical superharmonic function in
\Omega
; in particular,
\phi_{1}^{s}
is a classical superharmonic function for all
s\in(0,1)
.

(4)

Let
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
be open sets in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\phi_{i}
be a classical superharmonic function in
\Omega_{i}
, for
i=1,\,2
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\liminf_{x\rightarrow x_{1},x\in\Omega_{2}}\phi_{2}(x)\geq\phi_{1}(x_{1})\quad

\displaystyle\text{for all}\quad x_{1}\in\Omega_{1}\cap\partial\Omega_{2}\,;

\displaystyle\liminf_{x\rightarrow x_{2},x\in\Omega_{1}}\phi_{1}(x)\geq\phi_{2}(x_{2})\quad

\displaystyle\text{for all}\quad x_{2}\in\Omega_{2}\cap\partial\Omega_{1}\,.

Then the function

\displaystyle\phi(x):=\begin{cases}\phi_{1}(x)&\quad x\in\Omega_{1}\setminus\Omega_{2}\\
\phi_{1}(x)\wedge\phi_{2}(x)&\quad x\in\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}\\
\phi_{2}(x)&\quad x\in\Omega_{2}\setminus\Omega_{1}\\
\end{cases}

is also a classical superharmonic function in
\Omega 1\cup\Omega_{2}
.

The proof of Lemma
5.1
is as follows:
(1) follows directly from the definition of classical superharmonic functions; (2) and (3) are proved in
[
4
, Theorem 3.7.5, Corollary 3.4.4]
, respectively; (4) follows from
[
4
, Corollary 3.2.4]
.

Prior to discussing various domain conditions, it is worth recalling that the fat exterior condition and the thin exterior condition are among the most well-known criteria ensuring the Hardy inequality.
These conditions are closely related to the geometric properties of
\Omega
, specifically the Hausdorff dimension and the Aikawa dimension of
\Omega^{c}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

A domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
admits the Hardy inequality (
1.3
) if one of the following conditions holds:

(1)

(Fat exterior condition) There exist constants
s>d-2
and
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\infty}\big(\Omega^{c}\cap\overline{B}(p,r)\big)\geq cr^{s}\quad\text{for all }p\in\partial\Omega\,\,\text{and}\,\,r>0\,.

(5.1)

Here,
\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{s}(E)
is the Hausdorff content of
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
defined by

\mathcal{H}_{\infty}^{s}(E):=\inf\Big\{\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}r_{i}^{s}\,:\,E\subset\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}B(x_{i},r_{i})\quad\text{where }x_{i}\in E\text{ and }r_{i}>0\Big\}\,.

(2)

(Thin exterior condition)
\beta_{0}:=\dim_{\mathcal{A}}(\Omega^{c})<d-2
, where
\dim_{\mathcal{A}}(E)
denotes the Aikawa dimension of
E
, defined as the infimum of
\beta\geq 0
for which there exists a constant
A_{\beta}
such that

\sup_{p\in E,\,r>0}\frac{1}{r^{\beta}}\int_{B_{r}(p)}\frac{1}{d(x,E)^{d-\beta}}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq A_{\beta}<\infty\,,

with considering
\frac{1}{0}=+\infty
.

Moreover, if the first condition holds, then the constant
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
in (
1.3
) can be chosen to depend only on
d
,
s
, and
c
.
If the second condition holds, then
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
can be chosen to depend only on
d
,
\beta_{0}
, and
\{A_{\beta}\}_{\beta>\beta_{0}}
.

We refer the reader to
[
68
]
for further details and discussion of this dimensional dichotomy.

Remark 5.3
.

The Aikawa dimension is equivalent to the Assouad dimension, which is defined in terms of a covering property (see
[
46
, Theorem 1.1]
).
For any
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the inequality
\dim_{\mathcal{H}}(E)\leq\dim_{\mathcal{A}}(E)
holds,
and the equality need not hold in general (see
[
45
, Section 2.2]
).
However, if
E
is Ahlfors regular, for example, if
E
is a smooth manifold or has a self-similar structure such as the Cantor set or the Koch snowflake, then
\dim_{\mathcal{H}}(E)=\dim_{\mathcal{A}}(E)
; see
[
45
, Lemma 2.1]
and
[
52
, Theorem 4.14]
.

5.1.
Domains with fat exterior: Harmonic measure decay property

We begin by recalling several conditions equivalent to the fat exterior condition.
For
p\in\partial\Omega
and
r>0
, we denote by
w(\,\cdot\,,p,r):=w\big(\,\cdot\,,\Omega\cap B_{r}(p),\Omega\cap\partial B_{r}(p)\big)
the
harmonic measure
of
\Omega\cap\partial B_{r}(p)
over
\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)
, which is defined as the Perron-Wiener-Brelot solution
u
of the equation

\displaystyle\Delta u=0\quad\text{in}\,\,\,\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)\quad;\quad u=1_{\Omega\cap\partial B_{r}(p)}\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\partial\big(\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)\big)\,.

In other words,

\displaystyle\begin{split}w(x,p,r):=\inf\big\{\phi(x)\,:\,\,&\text{$\phi$ is a classical superharmonic function in $\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)$}\\
&\,\,\text{and $\underset{y\rightarrow z}{\liminf}\,\phi(y)\geq 1_{\Omega\cap\partial B_{r}(p)}(z)$ for all $z\in\partial\big(\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)\big)$}\big\}\,.\end{split}

(5.2)

(see Figure
5.1
below).
Note that
\Omega\cap\partial B_{r}(p)
is a relatively open subset of
\partial\big(\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)\big)
.

\Delta u=0
u=1
u=0

Figure 5.1.
u:=w(\,\cdot\,,p,r)

Definition 5.4
.

A domain
\Omega
is said to satisfy the
local harmonic measure decay property
with exponent
\lambda>0
(abbreviated as ‘
\mathbf{LHMD}(\lambda)
’), if there exists a constant
M_{\lambda}>0
such that for any
p\in\partial\Omega
and
r>0
,

\displaystyle w(x,p,r)\leq M_{\lambda}\left(\frac{|x-p|}{r}\right)^{\lambda}\quad\text{for all}\,\,\,x\in\Omega\cap B(p,r)

(5.3)

Remark 5.5
.

\mathbf{LHMD}
is closely related to the Hölder continuity of the PWB solutions.
For the moment, we assume that
\Omega
is bounded and satisfies the following property:

For any
f\in C(\partial\Omega)
, the PWB solution of the equation

\Delta u=0\quad\text{in}\,\,\,\Omega\quad;\quad u=f\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\partial\Omega

coincides with the classical solution; that is,
u\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)\cap C(\overline{\Omega})
and the equation holds pointwise.

Here, the PWB solution
u
is defined as in (
5.2
), by replacing
\big(\Omega\cap B_{r}(p),1_{\Omega\cap\partial B_{r}(p)}\big)
with
(\Omega,f)
.
We denote this solution
u
by
H_{\Omega}f
.
For
\lambda\in(0,1]
, we denote by
\|H_{\Omega}\|_{\lambda}
the operator norm of
H_{\Omega}
from
C^{0,\lambda}(\partial\Omega)
to
C^{0,\lambda}(\Omega)
,
i.e.
,

\|H_{\Omega}\|_{\lambda}:=\sup_{f\in C^{0,\lambda}(\partial\Omega),f\not\equiv 0}\frac{\|H_{\Omega}f\|_{C^{0,\lambda}(\Omega)}}{\|f\|_{C^{0,\lambda}(\partial\Omega)}}\,.

It is shown in
[
2
, Theorems 2 and 3]
that
\|H_{\Omega}\|_{1}=\infty
. Moreover, for
\lambda\in(0,1)
, if
\|H_{\Omega}\|_{\lambda}<\infty
then
\Omega
satisfies
\mathbf{LHMD}(\lambda)
.
Conversely, if
\Omega
satisfies
\mathbf{LHMD}(\lambda^{\prime})
for some
\lambda^{\prime}>\lambda
, then
\|H_{\Omega}\|_{\lambda}<\infty
.

Example 5.6

(Section 5 in
[
62
]
)
.

(1)

Every convex domain satisfies
LHMD
(
1
), where
M_{1}
in (
5.3
) depends only on
d
.

(2)

Let
\Omega
be a domain satisfying the totally vanishing exterior Reifenberg condition (see Definition
5.16
).
Then for any
\epsilon>0
,
\Omega
satisfies
LHMD
(
1-\epsilon
), where
M_{1-\epsilon}
in (
5.3
) depends only on
d
,
\epsilon
,
\{R_{0,\delta}/R_{\infty,\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
.
Here,
R_{0,\delta}
and
R_{\infty,\delta}
are constants in (
5.46
).

(3)

For
\delta>0
, we denote by
\Lambda_{\delta}
the first eigenvalue of the Dirichlet spherical Laplacian on
\{\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{d})\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\,:\,\sigma_{1}>-\cos\delta\}
.
We also define

\displaystyle\lambda_{\delta}:=-\frac{d-2}{2}+\sqrt{\Big(\frac{d-2}{2}\Big)^{2}+\Lambda_{\delta}}\,.

(5.4)

In particular, when
d=2
, we set
\lambda_{0}=\frac{1}{2}
.
It is worth mentioning that
\lambda_{\delta}=\frac{\pi}{2(\pi-\delta)}
if
d=2
,
\lambda_{\delta}=\frac{\delta}{\pi-\delta}
if
d=4
, and
\lambda_{\delta}>0
for all
d\geq 3
and
\delta>0
; for more information of
\lambda_{\delta}
, see
[
5
,
16
]
.
Let
\delta\geq 0
if
d=2
, and
\delta>0
if
d\geq 3
, and suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a bounded domain satisfying the exterior
(\delta,R)
-cone condition,
0<R\leq\infty
.
Here, the exterior
(\delta,R)
-cone condition is that for every
p\in\partial\Omega
, there exists a unit vector
e_{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\displaystyle\{x\in B_{R}(p)\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{p}\geq|x-p|\cos\delta\}\subset\Omega^{c}\,.

(see Figure
5.2
below).

A. Lipschitz boundary
condition
B. Exterior
(\frac{\pi}{3},\infty)
-cone condition
(does not satisfy Lipschitz
boundary conddition)
C. Exterior
(0,\infty)
-cone condition
(does not satisfy
(\delta,R)
-cone
condition,
\forall\,\,\,\delta,\,R>0
)

Figure 5.2.
Examples for the exterior cone condition

Then
\Omega
satisfies
LHMD
(
\lambda_{\delta}
), where
M_{\lambda_{\delta}}
in (
5.3
) depends only on
d
,
\delta
, and
\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)/R
.
Moreover, if
\Omega
is an unbounded domain satisfying the
(\delta,\infty)
-exterior cone condition, then
\Omega
also satisfies
LHMD
(
\lambda_{\delta}
).

Here is a relation between the fat exterior condition and
LHMD
(
\lambda
), which is stated in
[
3
, Lemma 3]
and
[
35
, Theorem 7.22]
(or, see
[
62
, Lemma 4.10]
).

Proposition 5.7
.

The following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

There exists
s>d-2
such that the fat exterior condition (
5.1
) holds.

(2)

There exists
\lambda>0
such that
\mathbf{LHMD}(\lambda)
holds.

(3)

There exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{p\in\partial\Omega,r>0}\frac{\mathrm{Cap}\big(\Omega^{c}\cap\overline{B}(p,r),B(p,2r)\big)}{\mathrm{Cap}\big(\overline{B}(p,r),B(p,2r)\big)}\geq\epsilon_{0}>0\,.

(5.5)

Here,
\mathrm{Cap}\big(K,B\big)
is the capacity of a compact set
K\subset B
relative to an open ball
B
, defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cap}(K,B):=\inf\left\{\|\nabla f\|_{2}^{2}\,:\,f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B)\,\,\,,\,\,\,f\geq 1\,\,\text{on}\,\,K\right\}\,.

(5.6)

In particular, constants
(c,s)
in (
5.1
),
(\lambda,M_{\lambda})
in (
5.3
), and
\epsilon_{0}
in (
5.5
) depend only on another and
d
.

A well-known sufficient condition for (
5.5
) to hold is that

\displaystyle\inf_{p\in\partial\Omega,r>0}\frac{|\Omega^{c}\cap\overline{B}(p,r)|}{|\overline{B}(p,r)|}\geq\epsilon_{1}>0

(5.7)

(see,
e.g.
,
[
35
, Example 6.18]
).
For further discussion of the capacity density condition, see
[
36
,
47
]
and the references therein.

In view of Proposition
5.7
, we consider domains satisfying
\mathbf{LHMD}(\lambda)
for some
\lambda>0
, instead of (
5.1
).
This assumption is implied by certain geometric conditions, as shown in Example
5.6
.

The following lemma is presented in
[
62
, Theorem 4.12]
:

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\Omega
satisfy
\mathbf{LHMD}(\lambda)
,
\lambda>0
.
Then for any
\lambda^{\prime}\in(0,\lambda)
, there exists a superharmonic function
\phi
in
\Omega
such that

N^{-1}\rho(x)^{\lambda^{\prime}}\leq\phi(x)\leq N\rho(x)^{\lambda^{\prime}}\,,

where
N=N(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},M_{\lambda})>0
.

Combining Lemma
5.8
with Theorem
4.8
, we obtain the following result:

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfy
\mathbf{LHMD}(\lambda)
,
\lambda>0
, and consider the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\Delta u+f\quad\text{in}\,\,(0,T]\,,

(5.8)

where
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
0<T<\infty
.
Then for any
p\in(1,\infty)
and
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle-2-(p-1)\lambda<\theta-d<-2+\lambda\,,

and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, the following statements hold:

(1)

For any
f\in\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)
, equation (
5.8
)
has a unique solution
u
in
\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}\leq N\|f\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)}\,,

(5.9)

where
N=N(d,p,\alpha,\gamma,\theta,\lambda,M_{\lambda})
.

(2)

If
\frac{1}{p}<\alpha
, then for any
u_{0}\in B^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}_{p,\theta+2/\alpha}(\Omega)
and
f\in\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)
, equation (
5.8
) with
u(0,\cdot)=u_{0}

has a unique solution
u
in
\mathcal{H}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}\leq N\left(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{\gamma+2-2/(p\alpha)}_{p,\theta+2/\alpha}(\Omega)}+\|f\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)}\right)\,,

(5.10)

where
N=N(d,p,\alpha,\gamma,\theta,\lambda,M_{\lambda})
.

Proof.

Take
\lambda^{\prime}\in(0,\lambda)
such that

-2-(p-1)\lambda^{\prime}<\theta-d<-2+\lambda^{\prime}.

Lemma
5.2
and Proposition
5.7
imply that
\Omega
admits the Hardy inequality (
1.3
), where
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
can be chosen to depend only on
d
,
\alpha
, and
M_{\alpha}
(in (
5.3
)).
By Lemma
5.8
, there exists a superharmonic Harnack function
\phi
, such that
\widetilde{\rho}^{\,\lambda^{\prime}}
is a regularization of
\phi
.
In addition,
\mathrm{C}_{3}(\phi,\widetilde{\rho}^{\,\lambda^{\prime}})
depends only on
d
,
\lambda
,
\lambda^{\prime}
,
M_{\lambda}
.
Therefore, applying Theorems
4.8
with
\Psi:=\widetilde{\rho}^{\,\lambda^{\prime}}
(see Proposition
3.2
.(1) and (
2.23
)), the proof is complete.
∎

We next present further results for domains with a fat exterior.
The first result is an unweighted
L_{p}
solvability result when
p
is close to
2
.
The second result concerns the H”older regularity of solutions in domains that satisfy the capacity density condition (
5.5
).

We denote by
\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)
the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
in

W_{p}^{1}(\Omega):=\big\{f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)\,:\,\|f\|_{W_{p}^{1}(\Omega)}:=\|f\|_{p}+\|\nabla f\|_{p}<\infty\big\}\,.

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
\Omega
satisfy the capacity density condition (
5.5
).
Then, for any
0<\nu_{1}\leq\nu_{2}<\infty
, there exists
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, depending only on
d
,
\epsilon_{0}
(in (
5.5
)),
\nu_{1}
,
\nu_{2}
, such that the following holds:

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
and consider the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u+f^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}\quad\text{in}\quad(0,T]\quad;\quad u(0,\cdot)=0

(5.11)

in the sense of (
4.2
).
If
p\in(2-\epsilon,2+\epsilon)
and
f^{0},\,\ldots,\,f^{d}\in L_{p}\big((0,T]\times\Omega\big)
, then equation (
5.11
)
has a unique solution
u
in
L_{p}\big((0,T];\mathring{W}^{1}_{p}(\Omega)\big)
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\nabla u\|_{L_{p}((0,T]\times\Omega)}+\min(T^{\alpha/2},D_{\Omega})^{-1}\|u\|_{L_{p}((0,T]\times\Omega)}\\
\lesssim_{d,p,\epsilon_{0}}\,&\min(T^{\alpha/2},D_{\Omega})\|f^{0}\|_{L_{p}((0,T]\times\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|f^{i}\|_{L_{p}((0,T]\times\Omega)}\,,\end{split}

(5.12)

where
D_{\Omega}:=\sup_{x\in\Omega}d(x,\partial\Omega)
.

Proof.

We say that
Sol(
p,\theta
)
holds if the following assertion is true:

For any
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
and
f\in\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)
,
the equation

\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u+f\quad\text{in}\,\,(0,T]

has a unique solution
u
in
\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
.
Moreover, there exists a constant
N>0
, depending only on
d
,
p
,
\theta
,
\alpha
,
\nu_{1}
,
\nu_{2}
, and
\epsilon_{0}
in (
5.5
), such that

\|u\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}\leq N\|f\|_{\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)}\,.

We first note the following two results for the capacity density condition:

(a)

By Proposition
5.7
and Lemma
5.2
,
\Omega
admits the Hardy inequality (
1.3
), where
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
can be chosen to depend only on
d
and
\epsilon_{0}
.
Moreover, by Lemma
5.8
, there exists
\lambda^{\prime}>0
and a superharmonic function
\phi
in
\Omega
such that
\psi\simeq_{d,\epsilon_{0}}\rho^{\lambda^{\prime}}
,
where
{\lambda^{\prime}}
depends only on
d
and
\epsilon_{0}
.
By Theorem
4.8
(with
\Psi=\widetilde{\rho}^{\lambda^{\prime}}
) and Proposition
3.2
.(1), if
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
satisfies

\displaystyle-\frac{(p-1)\lambda^{\prime}}{p\big(\sqrt{\nu_{2}/\nu_{1}}-1\big)/2+1}<\theta-d+2<\frac{(p-1)\lambda^{\prime}}{p\big(\sqrt{\nu_{2}/\nu_{1}}+1\big)/2-1}\,,

(5.13)

then
Sol(
p,\theta
)
holds.
The first term in (
5.13
) goes to
-\lambda^{\prime}\sqrt{\nu_{1}/\nu_{2}}
as
p\rightarrow 2
, while the second term in (
5.13
) goes to
\lambda^{\prime}\sqrt{\nu_{1}/\nu_{2}}
as
p\rightarrow 2
.
Therefore, there exists
\epsilon_{1}>0
(depending only on
\nu_{1}
,
\nu_{2}
, and
\alpha
) such that if
p\in(2-\epsilon_{1},2+\epsilon_{1})
, then
\theta:=d-p
satisfies (
5.13
), and thus
Sol(
p,d-p
)
holds.

(b)

It is implied by
[
47
, Theorem 1, Theorem 2]
(or see
[
36
, Theorem 3.7, Corollary 3.11]
) that there exists
\epsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
depending only on
d
and
\epsilon_{0}
such that for any
p>2-\epsilon_{2}
, if
u\in\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)
, then

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\Big|\frac{u(x)}{\rho(x)}\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq N(d,p,\epsilon_{0})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\quad\forall\,\,u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)\,.

(5.14)

This implies that
L_{p,d}(\Omega)\cap H_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega)\subset\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)
.
Indeed,
L_{p,d}(\Omega)\cap H_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega)
is continuously embedded in
W_{p}^{1}
, and
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is dense in
L_{p,d}(\Omega)\cap H_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega)
(see Lemma
2.16
).

Fix
0<\epsilon<\min(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
and consider
p\in(2-\epsilon,2+\epsilon)
.
Then for any
p\in(2-\epsilon,2+\epsilon)
,
Sol(
p,d-p
)
holds and
\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)\subset H_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega)
.

Step 1. Uniqueness of the solution.

Suppose that
u\in L_{p}\big((0,T];\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)\big)
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u\quad\text{in}\quad(0,T]\quad;\quad u(0,\cdot)\equiv 0\,.

(5.15)

Since
\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)\subset H_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega)
(see (
5.14
) and (
2.10
)), we have
L_{p}\big((0,T];\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)\big)\subset\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,T)
.
Consequently,
u\in\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,T)
and
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\mathcal{L}u\in\mathbb{H}_{p,d+p}^{-1}(\Omega,T)
.
Since (
4.7
) holds, we have
u\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,T)
.
Since
Sol(
p,d-p
)
holds, the solution of (
5.15
) is unique in
\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,T)
, and therefore
u\equiv 0
.

Step 2. Existence of solutions and estimate (
5.12
).

To prove the existence of solutions, it is enough to find a solution in
\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,T)\cap\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,T)
.
Indeed, if
u\in\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,T)\cap\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,T)
, then there exists
u_{n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\Omega)
such that
u_{n}\rightarrow u
in
\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,T)\cap\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,T)
(see Lemma
4.6
).
Since
L_{p,d}(\Omega)\cap H_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega)\subset\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)
,
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L_{p}\big((0,T];\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)\big)
, so that
u=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}u_{n}\in L_{p}\big((0,T];\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)\big)
.
Hence,
\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,T)\cap\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,T)\subset L_{p}\big((0,T];\mathring{W}_{p}^{1}(\Omega)\big)
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
T=1
by dilation.
Note that
\epsilon_{0}
in (
5.5
) is invariant even if
\Omega
is replaced by
r\Omega=\{rx\,:\,x\in\Omega\}
for any
r>0
.

In this step, we use (
2.10
) and the inequalities

D_{\Omega}^{\,-1}\|u\|_{p}\leq\|\rho^{-1}u\|_{p}\simeq\|u\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d-p}(\Omega,1)}\quad\text{and}\quad\|f\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d+p}(\Omega,1)}\simeq\|\rho f\|_{p}\leq D_{\Omega}\|f\|_{p}

(see (
2.10
)) without mentioning.
Additionally, we employ the fact that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,1)}+\|u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega,1)}+\|u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d+p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim_{\alpha,\gamma,p,d}\|u\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,1)}\,.

(5.16)

The first term of LHS in (
5.16
) is estimated by the definition of
\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,\gamma+2}(\Omega,1)
.
For the third term of LHS in (
5.16
), note that for arbitrary
\widetilde{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,1)\big)
, by using (
4.2
) with
\eta:=\int_{t}^{1}\frac{(s-t)^{-1+\alpha}}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\widetilde{\eta}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\langle u(t),\phi\rangle\widetilde{\eta}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{1}I_{t}^{\alpha}\langle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u(t),\phi\rangle\widetilde{\eta}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,.

(5.17)

For RHS in (
5.17
), note that by a similar way with (
4.4
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\Big(I_{t}^{\alpha}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u(t)\|_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\Big)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim_{p,\alpha}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,1)}^{p}\,.

(5.18)

Hence
I_{t}^{\alpha}|\langle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u(t),\phi\rangle|
is well-defined as an element of
L_{p}\big((0,1]\big)
(see (
2.13
)).
Since (
5.17
) holds for all
\widetilde{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,1)\big)
, we obtain that for almost every
t\in(0,1]
,
\langle u(t),\phi\rangle=I_{t}^{\alpha}\langle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u(t),\phi\rangle
and

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{d,p,\theta,\gamma}I_{t}^{\alpha}\|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u(t)\|_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,,

(5.19)

(see (
2.13
)).
(
5.18
) and (
5.19
) imply the estimate for the third term of LHS in (
5.16
).
The second term of LHS in (
5.16
) is estimated by the first and third term of LHS in (
5.16
) (see (
2.26
)).
Therefore, (
5.16
) is proved.

Step 2.1)
Since
Sol(
p,d-p
)
holds, there exists
v\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,1)
such that
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}v=\mathcal{L}v+\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-1}f^{0}
and

\displaystyle\|v\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim_{d,p,\epsilon_{0}}\left\|\widetilde{\rho}^{-1}f^{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d+p}(\Omega,1)}\simeq_{p,d}\|f^{0}\|_{p}\,.

(5.20)

By (
5.16
) and (
5.20
), we have

\displaystyle\|v\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d}^{1}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim\|v\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim_{d,p,\epsilon_{0}}\|f^{0}\|_{p}\,.

(5.21)

Put

\widetilde{f}:=f^{0}-\partial_{t}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\rho}\,v)+\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\rho}\,v)=2\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{d}a_{ij}(t)D_{i}\big(vD_{i}\widetilde{\rho})\Big]-v\mathcal{L}\widetilde{\rho}\,,

and observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\widetilde{f}\big\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d+p}^{-1}(\Omega,1)}&\lesssim_{d,p}\|v\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim_{d,p}\|v\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d}^{1}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim_{d,p,\epsilon_{0}}\left\|f^{0}\right\|_{p}\,,\end{split}

where the first inequality follows from (
2.23
) and (
2.27
), while the third one follows from (
5.21
).
Since
Sol(
p,\theta
)
holds, there exists
w\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,1)
such that

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\partial_{t}^{\alpha}w=\mathcal{L}w+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}+\widetilde{f}\quad,\quad\text{and}\\
\|w\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim_{d,p,\epsilon_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|f^{i}\|_{L_{p,d}(\Omega)}+\big\|\widetilde{f}\big\|_{H_{p,d+p}^{-1}(\Omega)}\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{d}\|f^{i}\|_{p}\,.\end{gathered}

Put
u=\widetilde{\rho}\,v+w
.
Then
u
is a solution of equation (
5.11
) and satisfies

\displaystyle\|u_{x}\|_{p}+(1+D_{\Omega}^{-1})\|u\|_{p}\lesssim_{d,p}\|u\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,1)}+\|u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,p}\,

\displaystyle\|w\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,1)}+\|w\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,1)}+\|v\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d+p}(\Omega,1)}+\|v\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d}^{1}(\Omega,1)}

(5.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,p}\,

\displaystyle\|w\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,1)}+\|v\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,2}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim_{d,p,\epsilon_{0}}\sum_{i\geq 0}\|f^{i}\|_{p}\,,

where the last inequality follows from (
5.21
) and (
5.1
); note that (
5.24
) also implies that
u\in\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,1)\cap\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,1)
.

This result also completes the proof for the case
D_{\Omega}=\infty
.

Step 2.2)
Next, we consider the case
D_{\Omega}<\infty
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\Big\|f^{0}+\sum_{i\geq 1}D_{i}f^{i}\Big\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d+p}^{-1}(\Omega,1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,p}\,

\displaystyle\|f^{0}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d+p}(\Omega,1)}+\sum_{i\geq 1}\|f^{i}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,1)}

(5.25)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D_{\Omega}\|f^{0}\|_{p}+\sum_{i\geq 1}\|f^{i}\|_{p}<\infty\,.

Since
Sol(
p,d-p
)
holds, there exists
\widetilde{u}\in\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,1)
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\widetilde{u}=\mathcal{L}\widetilde{u}+f^{0}+\sum_{i\geq 1}D_{i}f^{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\widetilde{u}\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim\|f^{0}+\sum_{i\geq 1}D_{i}f^{i}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d+p}^{-1}(\Omega,1)}\,.

(5.26)

By (
5.16
), (
5.25
), and (
5.26
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\|_{p}+D_{\Omega}^{-1}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{p}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{p}\lesssim_{d,p}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,1)}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,d+p}^{-1}(\Omega,1)}

(5.27)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,p}

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{u}\|_{\mathring{\mathcal{H}}_{p,d-p}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,1)}\lesssim_{d,p,\epsilon_{0}}D_{\Omega}\|f^{0}\|_{p}+\sum_{i\geq 1}\|f^{i}\|_{p}\,.

By (
5.27
), we have
\widetilde{u}\in\mathbb{L}_{p,d}(\Omega,1)\cap\mathbb{H}_{p,d-p}^{1}(\Omega,1)
.

Since
\widetilde{u}
in Step 2.2 coincides with
u
in Step 2.1 (by the result in Step 1), (
5.12
) follows from (
5.24
) and (
5.27
).
∎

Theorem 5.11
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain satisfying
\mathbf{LHMD}(\lambda)
.
For
T\in(0,\infty)
and
0<\alpha\leq 1
, consider the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\Delta u+f_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f_{i}\quad\text{in}\quad(0,T]\times\Omega\,;

(5.28)

\displaystyle u(0,\cdot)=u_{0}\quad\text{and}\quad u|_{(0,T]\times\partial\Omega}\equiv 0\,,

(5.29)

where
u_{0}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
f_{i}:(0,T]\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
i=0,\,1,\,\ldots,\,d
, are measurable functions.
Here,
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u
in (
5.28
) is understood in the sense of (
4.3
), while (
5.29
) is interpreted pointwise.

(1)

If

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}I:=\sup_{x\in\Omega}\bigg(|\rho^{-\lambda}u_{0}|+|\rho^{-\lambda+1}\nabla u_{0}|\bigg)<\infty\quad\text{and}\\
F:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<t\leq T\\
x\in\Omega\end{subarray}}\bigg(|\rho^{-\lambda+2}f_{0}|+\sum_{i=1}^{d}|\rho^{-\lambda+1}f_{i}|\bigg)<\infty\,,\end{gathered}

then there exists a solution
u\in C([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})
of (
5.28
) and (
5.29
).
Moreover, for any
\epsilon\in(0,1]
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle|u(t,x)|+\rho(x)^{1-\epsilon}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq T\\
y\in B(x,\rho(x)/2)\end{subarray}}\frac{|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|}{|x-y|^{1-\epsilon}+|t-s|^{(1-\epsilon)\alpha/2}}\leq N(F+I)\cdot\rho(x)^{\lambda-\delta}\,,

(5.30)

where
N
depends only on
d
,
\alpha
,
\lambda
,
M_{\lambda}
, the upper bounds of
\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)
and
T
, and
\epsilon
.
In particular, for any
\lambda_{1}\in(0,\lambda)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t,s\in[0,T]\\
x,y\in\Omega\end{subarray}}\frac{|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|}{|x-y|^{\lambda_{1}}+(t-s)^{\lambda_{1}\alpha/2}}\lesssim F+I\,.

(5.31)

(2)

Let
\psi
be a bounded superharmonic Harnack function in
\Omega
.
If there exist constants
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
\delta\in(0,\lambda\epsilon]
such that

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}I:=\sup_{x\in\Omega}\bigg(|\psi^{-1+\epsilon}\rho^{-\delta}u_{0}|+|\psi^{-1-\epsilon}\rho^{1-\delta}\nabla u_{0}|\bigg)<\infty\quad\text{and}\\
F:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<t\leq T\\
x\in\Omega\end{subarray}}\bigg(|\psi^{-1+\epsilon}\rho^{2-\delta}f_{0}|+\sum_{i=1}^{d}|\psi^{-1+\epsilon}\rho^{1-\delta}f_{i}|\bigg)<\infty\,,\end{gathered}

then there exists a solution
u\in C([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})
of (
5.28
) and (
5.29
).
Moreover, for any
\delta_{1}\in[0,\delta)
,

\displaystyle|u(t,x)|\leq N(F+I)\cdot\psi(x)^{1-\epsilon}\rho(x)^{\delta_{1}}

(5.32)

for all
t\in[0,T]
and
x\in\Omega
, where
N
depends only on
d
,
\alpha
,
\lambda
,
M_{\lambda}
, the upper bounds of
\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)
and
T
,
\mathrm{C}_{1}(\psi)
,
\epsilon
,
\delta
,
\delta_{1}
.

Proof.

(1)
Step 1.
We first consider the case of
\epsilon=\delta\in(0,1]
, where
\epsilon
satisfies
\frac{d+2/\alpha}{\epsilon}>\frac{2}{\lambda}-1
.
For any
p\geq\frac{d+2/\alpha}{\epsilon}
and
d-p\lambda\leq\theta\leq d+\lambda-2
, we have
-2-(p-1)\lambda<\theta-d<-2+\lambda
.
In addition,

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\|f\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{-1}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|f_{i}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p,\theta+p}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{\frac{\theta-d}{p}+\lambda}\big(T\cdot|\Omega|\big)^{1/p}F\,,\\
\|u_{0}\|_{B_{p,\theta+2/\alpha}^{1-2/(\alpha p)}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{H_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}^{1}(\Omega)}\lesssim\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{\frac{\theta_{0}-d}{p_{0}}+\lambda}|\Omega|^{1/p_{0}}I\,,\end{gathered}

where
p_{0}:=\frac{d}{d+2/\alpha}p
and
\theta_{0}-d:=\frac{d}{d+2/\alpha}(\theta-d)
(see (
2.28
) and (
2.21
)).
Therefore, by Theorem
4.8
, there exists a solution
u\in\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{1}(\Omega,T)
of the equation
\partial_{t}^{\alpha}u=\Delta u+f
with
u(0)=u_{0}
such that

\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim F+I\,.

By the global uniqueness (Theorem
4.13
), these
u
coincide for all
p
and
\theta
in the above.

We first prove (
5.30
) in the case
t=s
.
By taking
p:=\frac{d+2/\alpha}{\epsilon}
and
\theta:=-p\lambda+d
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|u(t)|_{0,1-\epsilon}^{(-\lambda+\epsilon)}\lesssim\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u(t)\|_{B_{p,\theta+2/\alpha}^{1-2/(\alpha p)}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim F+I\,,

(5.33)

where the first and second inequalities follow from (
2.25
) and Proposition
4.7
, respectively.

Next, we prove (
5.30
) for the case
x=y
.
Take large enough
p>1
such that
p>\frac{d+2/\alpha}{\epsilon}
.
Put
\beta=\frac{1}{p}+(1-\epsilon)\frac{\alpha}{2}
and
\theta=-\frac{2}{\alpha}-(\lambda-\epsilon)p
, so that
\theta-d<-2+\lambda
.
Then by
1-2\beta/\alpha-d/p>0
and
\theta/p+2\beta/\alpha=1-\lambda
, it follows from (
2.25
) and Proposition
4.7
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|u(t)-u(s)|^{(1-\lambda)}_{0,0}\leq\,&|u(t)-u(s)|_{0,1-2\beta/\alpha-d/p}^{(\theta/p+2\beta/\alpha)}\lesssim\|u(t)-u(s)\|_{B_{p,\theta+2p\beta/\alpha}^{1-2\beta/\alpha}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim\,&|t-s|^{\beta-1/p}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\alpha,1}(\Omega,T)}\lesssim|t-s|^{(1-\epsilon)\alpha/2}\big(F+I\big)\end{split}

(5.34)

Combining (
5.33
) and (
5.34
) yields (
5.30
).

Step 2.
For general
\epsilon\in(0,1]
and
\delta>0
, take
\epsilon_{0}>0
small enough such that
\epsilon_{0}\leq\min(\epsilon,\delta)
and
\frac{d+2/\alpha}{\epsilon_{0}}>\frac{2}{\lambda}-1
.
Since
\epsilon_{0}\leq\delta
, we have

|u(t,x)|\lesssim(F+I)\rho(x)^{\lambda-\epsilon_{0}}\leq\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{\delta-\epsilon_{0}}(F+I)\rho(x)^{\lambda-\delta}.

Moreover, for
y\in B\big(x,\rho(x)/2\big)
, since
\frac{\rho(x)}{2}\leq\rho(y)\leq 2\rho(x)
, we have

\displaystyle\rho(x)^{1-\epsilon}\frac{|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|}{|x-y|^{1-\epsilon}+|t-s|^{(1-\epsilon)\alpha/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\big(|u(t,x)|+|u(s,y)|\big)^{(\epsilon-\epsilon_{0})/(1-\epsilon_{0})}\left(\rho(x)^{1-\epsilon_{0}}\frac{|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|}{|x-y|^{1-\epsilon_{0}}+|t-s|^{(1-\epsilon_{0})\alpha/2}}\right)^{(1-\epsilon)/(1-\epsilon_{0})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle(F+I)\rho(x)^{\lambda-\epsilon_{0}}\leq\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{\delta-\epsilon_{0}}(F+I)\rho(x)^{\lambda-\delta}\,.

Step 3.
It remains to prove (
5.31
).
Without loss of generality, assume that
\rho(x)\geq\rho(y)
.
In (
5.30
), put
\epsilon=1-\lambda_{1}
and
\delta=\lambda-\lambda_{1}
.
Then we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq t\leq T\\
x\in\Omega\end{subarray}}\rho(x)^{-\lambda_{1}}|u(t,x)|+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t,s\in[0,T]\\
x\in\Omega,|x-y|<\rho(x)/2\end{subarray}}\frac{|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|}{|x-y|^{\lambda_{1}}+(t-s)^{\lambda_{1}\alpha/2}}\lesssim F+I\,.

(5.35)

Therefore it suffices to consider the case
|x-y|>\frac{\rho(x)}{2}
.
If
|x-y|\geq\frac{\rho(x)}{2}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{|u(t,x)-u(s,y)|}{|x-y|^{\lambda_{1}}+|t-s|^{\alpha\lambda_{1}/2}}\lesssim_{\lambda_{1}}\frac{|u(t,x)|+|u(t,y)|}{\rho(x)^{\lambda_{1}}}\leq\frac{|u(t,x)|}{\rho(x)^{\lambda_{1}}}+\frac{|u(t,y)|}{\rho(y)^{\lambda_{1}}}\lesssim F+I\,,

where the last inequality follows from (
5.35
).

(2) Since
0\leq\delta_{1}<\delta\leq\lambda\epsilon
, there exists
\lambda_{1}\in(0,\lambda)
, such that
\delta_{1}<\lambda_{1}\epsilon
.
Take a sufficiently large
p
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}<\min\Big(\frac{\lambda_{1}\epsilon-\delta_{1}}{d-2+2/\alpha+\lambda_{1}},\frac{\delta-\delta_{1}}{d+2/\alpha}\Big)\,,

(5.36)

and put

\mu:=1-\epsilon+\frac{\delta_{1}}{\lambda_{1}}+\frac{d-2+2/\alpha}{\lambda_{1}p}\quad,\quad r:=\frac{1-\epsilon}{\mu}\,.

Then we have

\mu\in(0,1-1/p\big)\,\,,\,\,\,\,r\in[0,1]\,\,,\,\,\,\,\mu r=1-\epsilon\,\,,\,\,\,\,\lambda_{1}\mu(1-r)=\frac{d-2+2/\alpha}{p}+\delta_{1}\,.

By Theorem
5.8
, there exists a superharmonic function
s
satisfying
s\simeq\rho^{\lambda_{1}}
.
Set
\Psi:=\widetilde{\rho}^{\,\lambda_{1}(1-r)}\widetilde{\psi}^{r}
, which is a regularization of the superharmonic function
s^{1-r}\psi^{r}
(see Lemma
5.1
.(3)).
Note that

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{1}(\Omega,T)=\widetilde{\psi}^{1-\epsilon}\widetilde{\rho}^{\lambda_{1}\mu(1-r)}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{1}(\Omega,T)=\widetilde{\psi}^{\,1-\epsilon}\mathcal{H}_{p,-2/\alpha-\delta_{1}p}^{1}(\Omega,T)\,,\\
\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}_{p,d+2p-2}^{-1}(\Omega,T)=\widetilde{\psi}^{\,1-\epsilon}\mathbb{H}_{p,2p-2/\alpha-\delta_{1}p}^{-1}(\Omega,T)\,,\\
\Psi^{\mu}B_{p,d-2+2/\alpha}^{1-2/(\alpha p)}(\Omega)=\widetilde{\psi}^{1-\epsilon}B_{p,-\delta_{1}p}^{1-2/(\alpha p)}(\Omega)\subset\widetilde{\psi}^{1-\epsilon}H^{1}_{p_{0},-\delta_{1}p_{0}}(\Omega)\end{gathered}

(see (
2.23
) and (
2.21
)), where
p_{0}=\frac{d}{d+2/\alpha}p
. (
2.10
) and (
2.28
) imply that

\displaystyle\big\|f^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}\big\|_{\Psi^{\mu}\mathbb{H}_{p,d+2p-2}^{-1}(\Omega,T)}+\big\|u_{0}\big\|_{\Psi^{\mu}B_{p,d-2+2/\alpha}^{1-2/(\alpha p)}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\big\|f^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}\big\|_{\widetilde{\psi}^{\,1-\epsilon}\mathbb{H}_{p,2p-2/\alpha-\delta_{1}p}^{-1}(\Omega,T)}+\big\|u_{0}\big\|_{\widetilde{\psi}^{1-\epsilon}H_{p_{0},-\delta_{1}p_{0}}^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{\delta-\delta_{1}-(d+2/\alpha)/p}\Big(\big(T\,|\Omega|\big)^{1/p}F+|\Omega|^{1/p_{0}}I\Big)\,.

Therefore Theorem
4.8
implies that there exists a solution

u\in\Psi^{\mu}\mathcal{H}_{p,d-2}^{1}(\Omega,T)=\widetilde{\psi}^{1-\epsilon}\mathcal{H}_{p,-2/\alpha-\delta_{1}p}^{1}(\Omega,T)

of equation (
5.28
).
By the global uniqueness, Theorem
4.13
, these
u
coincide for all
p
and
\mu
in the above.
Since
1-\frac{2}{\alpha p}-\frac{d}{p}>0
(see (
5.36
)), there exists
\beta_{t}>0
such that
1-\frac{2}{\alpha p}-\frac{d}{p}-\frac{2\beta_{t}}{\alpha}>0
.
(
2.25
) and Proposition
4.7
imply that for any
0\leq s<t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\frac{\big|\widetilde{\psi}^{-1+\epsilon}\big(u(t)-u(s)\big)\big|_{0,1-2/(\alpha p)-d/p-2\beta_{t}/\alpha}^{(-\delta_{1}+2\beta_{t}/\alpha)}}{|t-s|^{\beta_{t}}}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\|u(t)-u(s)\|_{\widetilde{\psi}^{(1-\epsilon)}B_{p,-\delta_{1}p+2p\beta_{t}/\alpha}^{1-2\beta_{t}/\alpha-2/(\alpha p)}}}{|t-s|^{\beta_{t}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{\widetilde{\psi}^{1-\epsilon}\mathcal{H}_{p,-2/\alpha-\delta_{1}p}^{1}(\Omega,T)}\,.

This shows that
u\in C([0,T]\times\Omega)
.
Moreover, by applying (
2.25
) and Proposition
4.7
again, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\big|\widetilde{\psi}^{-1+\epsilon}u(t)\big|_{0,1-2/(\alpha p)-d/p}^{(-\delta_{1})}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u(t)\|_{\widetilde{\psi}^{1-\epsilon}B_{p,0}^{1-2/(\alpha p)}(\Omega)}
